For hosted and delegated workers, the owner wrapper retains/hashes the original native result and extracts its full ID; the returned worker manifest also starts with `MODEL: <full-id>`, which must agree with that native result but cannot replace it. Conflicting IDs across the invocation, missing native fields, alias-only or unknown native values deny effects. The candidate/closure reviewer keeps the canonical `MODEL:` records and the same native provenance check. An interactive host performing a protected role itself needs an equivalent host-provided native machine-readable identity record; without one it can report/prepare, or explicitly orchestrate a bound external worker, but cannot attest its own picker/session label as proof. Never infer a full ID from requested configuration, a model picker, a body claim or another session's model. Current-run wrappers check actual native artifacts before effects; the hosted durable-adoption gate checks the committed historical source/identity attestation as already described.

### Legacy proposals at cutover

The consumer owns protected `legacy_proposals` settings: observed legacy actor identities and branch namespaces plus an explicit ID inventory at `.vh-adlc/docs-drift/legacy-proposals.yaml`. Record the legacy source/workflow identity, observed PR IDs/heads/body digests and scope without copying proposal bodies into the shared repo. Collect that complete non-mutating inventory after legacy workflow drainage and the operator's interactive freeze, before final C paired review; if an earlier C was reviewed before the inventory stabilized, refresh/review C again. D still adds only its adoption record. This inventories existing work; it neither resolves, rewrites nor closes legacy PRs during migration.

First and later shared audits collect the recorded IDs and all current open proposals matching the configured legacy identities/namespaces, alongside shared proposals. A legacy PR without a shared receipt is never falsely treated as a reviewed shared delivery or given a fabricated backfilled receipt. Recheck its proposed corrections even when no fresh correction touches those paths: already fixed differently, now wrong/unsupported or not currently verifiable becomes a linked stale-proposal needs-resolution decision. It is new attention on first surfacing and carryable through a verified earlier report when unchanged, never an unqualified no-change or an instruction to replay the old patch. Fresh correction overlap on the same path becomes a needs-resolution content decision with the existing PR linked, and no competing branch/PR is published for those paths. Non-overlapping verified corrections may proceed under the normal selected scope. Newly discovered or materially changed legacy proposals are reported; incomplete required collection still fails readiness. After the first complete report surfaces the overlap decision, an unchanged next run can carry it through the verified prior-report attention rule even though the old PR itself has no shared receipt. A separately selected resolve may reverify/consolidate those originals and publish a new reviewed binding; superseded closure still waits for its merged consolidation and fresh closure checks. No automatic backlog migration or exception to the merge-first rule is introduced.

### Durable reviewed publication bindings

Every scheduled and interactive publisher creates a retained immutable operation ref under the protected `publication.receipt_ref_prefix`, for example `refs/heads/docs-drift-receipts/<operation-id>`, before its PR/issue/comment effects. This is separate from the ephemeral cooperative lease. The current request/adopted grant must name its exact create-and-retain namespace; absent that permission, hand off before publication rather than silently adding a branch. Use an evidence-only Git commit/tree containing the canonical immutable intent/review/payload binding, with no application files, executable workflow, credentials or document bodies. The ref points to the sealed plan object, not a mutable status file, and release of the lease never removes it. Create only when absent, verify an existing identical object for unchanged recovery and refuse replacement/force updates. Retirement needs separately authorized cleanup after every linked proposal/recovery need is finished; it is not ordinary lease release or automatic merged-branch cleanup.

The PR's reviewed body includes only the preselected operation ID and deterministic receipt-ref locator before review. The intent binds that body digest, exact candidate head/tree, actor/repository, assessment/read-set bindings, individual correction identities and proposed-postimage/patch digests, carried-attention entries and native review verdict/model/digests. The plan contains neither its own future Git object ID nor a future PR ID. After sealing, the publisher creates the owned ref at the computed object and records its observed ID externally; no plan hash is inserted into the reviewed body afterward. This retains the existing operator-attestation limitation against deliberate same-user forgery rather than claiming an enforced proof controller.

A hosted reader derives only the configured same-repository ref from the validated operation marker, then uses typed GET-only Git ref/commit/tree/blob reads to retrieve the bounded canonical plan. Verify ref/object integrity, the plan's repository/actor/head/tree/body and reviewed verdict bindings against the observed PR and current approved policy; never follow an arbitrary PR-body clone/download URL. Thus interactive consolidation PRs are readable from the hosted route without a host-local cache or a scheduled producer having copied the receipt. Producer reports must retain the operation/ref/object locator for their published effects, but the native retained ref is the authoritative hosted source for that binding. Missing or changed refs/plans cannot be reconstructed from PR text and cannot count as carried evidence. An interrupted receipt-ref create is reconciled by exact observed ownership/object before proceeding; unrelated refs are never reused or deleted.

### Installed synthetic trial entry

The installed package includes `trial-entry.ts` as an explicit test-only entry, separate from production `cli.ts publish` and `resume-effects`. The retained consumer launcher accepts a literal `--synthetic-trial <context>` invocation only for this entry; the canonical installed procedure recognizes that invocation and routes its helper calls through the installed trial driver. It loads the same installed instructions, assessment/resolution helpers and common publisher API, without patching/copying the procedure or modifying the production CLI. The trial context is an immutable manifest in an owned synthetic Git fixture, binding its exact source, fixture tracker database, local bare Git origins, allowed synthetic IDs/output paths, test grant, component/client/launcher identities and collector observation directory. Reject real consumer origin/repository identity, real backlog IDs, non-owned paths/symlinks and any URL or ref outside that exact fixture context before collection/effects. It cannot select a production credential or network GitHub endpoint. Setup records the exact context and fixture ownership; reports label all observations synthetic.

`transport.ts` exposes injectable tracker-read, object-fetch, merge-observation and effect interfaces, with fixed native implementations in production. The GET-only `github-read.ts` remains the production reader unchanged; the trial driver injects fixture tracker reads at its caller boundary instead of spawning `gh api`. PR/base/merge objects are fetched only from the manifest-bound owned local bare fixture origin with the same hardened Git primitive; merge observations come from committed fixture tracker/Git state. The collector records exact attempted branch/PR/issue/comment/closure effects and supplies controlled interruption/merge states to the same publisher/recovery implementation. No PATH shadowing, environment endpoint override or fixture switch is exposed by production. Trial driver receipts bind the full context and entry/procedure/runtime identities; production gates reject them as live effect or hosted-trial proof.

Actual Copilot and Claude entry tests invoke their native installed front doors, retained launcher and this explicit test-only route, then run the installed audit/resolve/fresh-session closure/issue procedure against the fixture, including the explicitly approved delivery-acknowledgement recovery case and its ownership/settlement denials. Their claim is installed entry/loader dispatch and common procedure/publisher execution with synthetic transport, not execution of the production native transport. Separate production native-transport tests cover real command construction and deny collector context/flags on production entries; the actual hosted draft-PR trial covers the live GitHub advisory route. Test that a normal production invocation, report/settings/environment values and real consumer identity cannot reach the synthetic seam, and that the trial entry refuses every attempt to reach a real repository. This is a test boundary with explicit limitations, not a sandbox against an agent with unrestricted shell access.

### Capability evidence by host

The canonical operation is portable; available execution and reviewer capabilities differ. These are current evidence states for this new feature, not inherited claims from other operations. Record exact installed payload/host/model, scenario and transport in each cell as tests run. Installation/helper checks alone do not mark an actual model operation tested. A host unable to select/attest the adopted full worker/reviewer identity is limited to report/local preparation with a pending-review handoff; no fallback or implied real-corpus write support.

| Host | Report / local prepare | Bound independent reviewer dispatch | Interactive resolve publication | Post-merge closure | Unattended advisory worker | Issue publication |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Codex CLI | Unclaimed; native install/discovery/helper checks required | Unclaimed; test exact-model dispatch or the refusal/handoff | Unclaimed; positive claim needs actual installed operation and matching roles | Unclaimed; same requirement | Not a scheduled-run claim | Unclaimed; actual installed operation required |
| Claude Code CLI | Unclaimed; native installs/helpers and actual forwarded command required | Unclaimed; required external bound-role dispatch | Unclaimed; required actual `/docs-drift` synthetic collector invocation before cutover | Unclaimed; required forwarded fresh-session collector invocation | Not a scheduled-run claim | Unclaimed; same forwarding/issue collector contract |
| Copilot CLI | Unclaimed; required isolated native marketplace install/upgrade, named-skill discovery and bounded actual installed-model trial | Unclaimed; required actual named-model review | Unclaimed; required actual installed-model synthetic collector trial | Unclaimed; required fresh-session synthetic collector trial | Not a hosted-run claim | Unclaimed; required actual installed-model synthetic collector trial |
| Hosted runner | Unclaimed; stage of required actual advisory trial | Unclaimed; required exact-role identity in that trial | Unclaimed; advisory grant does not cover resolve | Unclaimed; separate adopted resume grant/trial required | Unclaimed; required actual runner/provider/draft-PR trial | Unclaimed; advisory grant does not cover issues |
| Codex desktop / VS Code GUI / phone surfaces | Unclaimed | Unclaimed | Unclaimed | Unclaimed | Not a scheduled-run claim | Unclaimed |

The required post-cutover interactive publishing entries are the installed `vh-adlc-docs-drift` operation invoked through Copilot CLI, and Stackweaver's existing Claude Code `/docs-drift` command forwarding to that operation with configured bound external worker/reviewer roles. Claude may dispatch those roles without being the protected worker itself; it must not silently perform them under an unbound model. Before D retires the legacy route, observe installed audit/resolve publication and fresh-session closure scenarios through the Copilot entry, and an actual bounded Claude command invocation exercising forwarded arguments, selected decisions, external role dispatch, synthetic publication and closure through the collector. Record Claude-native dispatch plus worker/reviewer provenance/results; a Copilot read of the command, installer discovery or static forwarding cannot substitute. Copilot remains the requested independent reviewer and main operation test worker; the Claude check tests the existing entry. If Claude is unavailable, retain both legacy publishing routes and leave cutover/AC-06 incomplete until a suitable host can run it. No token purchase or Claude invocation is authorized by this spec alone. Codex/other publishing entries remain unclaimed until exercised.

AC-01 proves discovery, setup and missing-capability refusal. AC-07 supplies the actual per-cell evidence: Codex/Claude native fresh installs/upgrades and helper checks; Copilot actual audit/resolve/closure/issue collector operations plus the required Claude daily-command dispatch through bound roles; and hosted actual advisory trial. Mark a collector-backed cell `tested (synthetic transport)` while leaving real GitHub resolve/closure unclaimed; the hosted draft-PR trial alone is live GitHub evidence. No positive operation/GUI/phone cell is claimed from installer tests or another host's trial. Codex operation cells may remain unclaimed; the specific Claude daily-command operation checks above are required before retiring that publishing route. Any later positive host claim needs that installed operation and reviewer/worker capability observed there. Unsupported reviewer/model selection must have a tested refusal before declaring that limitation handled.

Base freshness has a mechanical rule. Bind the assessment to its exact base commit and input read set: the configured document, source and reference roots (including negative/absence searches), settings, scanner/adapter, feedback, worker/procedure, generator and verification inputs, plus required Git-history inputs. Default to the whole tracked repository when that read set cannot be bounded completely. Under the lease, read the current consumer default and compare its full delta from the assessed base against that binding. A non-descendant base, unavailable comparison or any bound input change requires a fresh scan, verification and independent review. A current default advance disjoint from every bound input may be recorded as unrelated and does not invalidate the original reviewed checkpoint; do not rebase, regenerate or amend that checkpoint to follow it. Record both observed revisions and the disjoint delta in publication evidence. If a required history/time input changes, it is material even when paths are otherwise disjoint. Tests show an unrelated out-of-scope advance may proceed with the same reviewed head, while an in-scope/settings/feedback/verification/history advance cannot.

Production PR state is explicit consumer policy: `publication.pr_state.advisory` and `.resolve` each select `draft` or `ready`, preserving the consumer's observed current delivery policy during migration. Missing values stop setup; the shared workflow does not silently choose or change them. One common publisher receives a typed `pr_draft` input derived from that policy. Trial policy fixes `pr_draft: true` and records the corresponding production value as its sole PR-state difference. It changes only the PR-creation draft field, never the review, file-scope, merge, permission or closure gates. Tests assert both production values through the same publisher path and require the real trial's PR to be draft.

The single authoritative real-corpus publication gate is the `publication` block of committed `.vh-adlc/docs-drift.yaml` at the freshly observed consumer default branch. It names `mode: staged|adopted`, the exact publication lease ref and committed adoption-record path. Read that record as a Git blob from the same default commit and compute its digest at observation time; do not put the record's own digest into the settings it binds. Missing or staged configuration denies shared real-corpus push, PR, issue and closure effects even when a generic current request asks to publish. A publication request is not a cutover-state transition; changing the staged policy requires the explicit reviewed adoption procedure or a separate owner-authorized policy revision. Candidate-branch or dirty configuration, reports and cached receipts cannot replace current default policy.

`adopted` is necessary, not sufficient: the runtime gate checks the referenced committed adoption attestation's schema, recorded passing-trial and paired-review verdicts/identities/digests, exact current component/worker/reviewer/publisher bindings and expected route. Operator-time verification of the original artifact bytes is a separate prerequisite before authorized adoption delivery, as defined below. It does not require a post-cutover observation to permit the first adopted publication; the non-circular assembly and later completion evidence are defined below. The mode does not grant any effect; the actual request/adopted consumer grant is still required. Recheck policy and review bindings under the lease immediately before remote effects. Rollback treats this block and record reference as migration-owned: first quiesce shared publication under the same lease, then restore staged mode together with the legacy worker route through normal authorized consumer delivery. A previously adopted local candidate or cached receipt must then be denied by current-default policy. Tests cover missing/pre-cutover mode, a generic publish request before cutover, stale adopted candidate configuration after rollback, and denied shared effects when the legacy worker is restored.

Routing is explicit at every stage. Before cutover, the existing `.claude/commands/docs-drift.md` and its harness-neutral Stackweaver resolver entry continue to select the legacy resolver for its existing selected publishing/closure flow; the scheduled entry selects the legacy auditor. The new direct shared operation is available only for report/local preparation and has no real-corpus publishing route in this state. At cutover, the same reviewed consumer change that commits `publication.mode: adopted` switches both the interactive command and scheduled worker to shared forwarding instructions and disables/replaces every legacy real-corpus publication and closure entry. Frozen legacy instructions are rollback artifacts, not additional active agents. A current entry must refresh its committed policy/routing before effects; an obsolete cached instruction is not a supported alternative publisher. Post-cutover tests attempt the old interactive entry against the shared scheduled publisher and require shared forwarding/lease coordination or a refusal, never a concurrent legacy write. Rollback atomically restores staged policy and both legacy entries under the publication transition procedure.

During the staged interval the unchanged legacy publisher does not participate in the new lease. Therefore shared interactive adoption is report/local-prepare only: shared real-corpus push, advisory/resolve PR or issue publication and superseded-proposal closure are unavailable until the scheduled cutover. Keep the legacy publishing routes explicitly available under their existing consumer procedure; do not silently reconstruct or substitute a retired worker when setup fails. This is a declared migration state, not a ready publication mode. Test an active or newly starting legacy run against a shared resolve request and require denial of every shared real-corpus publication/closure effect. The only pre-cutover shared remote trial is the separately authorized synthetic, disjoint trial described below; it cannot touch real proposals or their namespaces.

After scheduled cutover, serialize the shared scheduled worker and a local resolve publisher for the same consumer through one cooperative repository publication lease. Only a run already authorized for the selected publication may create its named ephemeral lease ref; the adoption procedure names that acquire/release effect and its exact ref namespace. Atomically create the lease, record its exact object/run identity, and stop if another run owns it. Under the lease, recheck the current base, selected findings/PRs, exact reviewed candidate or issue payload, and current scope/authority before pushing, creating PRs/issues or explaining/closing superseded proposals. Release by an atomic comparison with the exact recorded lease object/run, only while still owned by that run; never force-take a foreign lease or infer ownership from its age. Interrupted or ambiguous ownership leaves a visible pending state for explicit reconciliation. This adds no merge, broad branch deletion, protection bypass or release authority. Authority and race/recovery tests cover the lease as well as the actual publication effects.

### Scheduled worker and staged adoption

Keep Stackweaver's schedule, selected provider/model and credentials local. Its future shared worker obtains the published vh-adlc source at an exact full commit corresponding to a verified release, loads the plugin directory through the provider's supported mechanism and verifies its component digest/version against the adopted installed payload. Put exact source repository/commit/release, plugin version and component SHA-256 in a separately committed immutable YAML pin under `.vh-adlc/docs-drift-components/<id>.yaml`, read through the existing strict reader. The reusable worker accepts an explicit `component_pin` path and validates its bytes against the selected adoption/trial manifest; there is no floating latest or report-supplied path. The active caller selects the approved pin, and a trial caller selects only its current-default trial-defined candidate pin. Track both source release and plugin version explicitly; they need not have the same version number. The component updater matches only a separate non-executing `.vh-adlc/docs-drift-upgrade-signal.yaml` source-SHA/release entry, with an extraction fixture. Protected provider-client updates use the separate non-executing signal entries defined below. Exclude every active/historical component pin file from custom-manager matching. The worker and runtime gate never read the signal as a pin. An updater PR is intake only and must never merge as-is or auto-merge: the selected successor procedure verifies the release, recomputes component digest/plugin metadata, creates a new immutable pin and prepares the reviewed pin/record selection together. Reject an updater edit to any retained pin, and keep approved/historical pins byte-identical. Updater proposals do not authorize automatic route changes or waive adoption/review checks.

`adoption.ts` exposes read-only signal consistency through `cli.ts adoption check-signal --source <commit>`. For an adopted source, resolve the valid selected record/pin and compare that pin's repository/source commit/release to the signal blob at the same immutable source. Mismatch returns nonzero with `signal-mismatch` and both identities; absent/malformed adopted selection cannot pass. In staged mode, a valid current-default `trial` block selects the comparison target: the exact trial-defined candidate component pin and protected client identities. Compare component/client signals to that target and return nonzero for mismatch, missing required signals or malformed trial bindings. Without a trial block, return exit 0 with explicit `staged-no-target` status, no comparison and no readiness/adoption claim; non-executing intake data cannot select or authorize a runtime. Any future adopted transition still requires its matching curated signals/pins/record. In adopted mode compare only the active approved selection, never a dormant trial candidate, so separately authorized trial setup cannot disable the approved runtime. These statuses grant no effects. Add this check to the consumer's existing mandatory PR/default CI verification, without changing branch protection, and scope the updater manager rule to the signal with automerge explicitly false. An intake-only PR changes only the signal and therefore fails the consistency check until transformed into a curated matching pin/record adoption; an owner-bypassed as-is merge is detected on default and reported. Scheduled adoption diagnostics also include that mismatch visibly. The signal is never used to select a runtime component: diagnostic drift neither grants a new version nor silently disables an otherwise valid approved pin. Tests cover the failing PR check, simulated bypass/default detection, continued old-pin selection, scoped automerge policy, and valid curated upgrade/rollback consistency.

Consumer adoption proceeds in three visible stages. First publish and marketplace-verify the shared component, then deliver the separately reviewed legacy-compatible scanner correction/separate protocol output, and only then adopt interactive report/local preparation while retaining all legacy real-corpus publishing routes. First adoption requires both the actual unattended advisory trial and the installed-model synthetic resolve/closure/issue collector trial defined below; neither substitutes for the other. Next, under separate publication authority, add a dispatch-only trial workflow such as `.github/workflows/docs-drift-shared-trial.yml` to the consumer's default branch; it has no cron trigger and does not replace the existing job. Finally, only after the observed trial, paired review and normal checks, switch both interactive and scheduled routes, retire/disable all legacy publication/closure entries and enable shared real-corpus publication in the same reviewed change. This is one shared implementation feature, with staged consumer adoption.

The real unattended trial means an actual hosted runner and selected provider/model, loading the exact published component and executing its assessment/preparation, independent candidate review and GitHub advisory handoff. Its inputs are a committed bounded synthetic documentation/code/feedback fixture in an isolated scratch Git repository, using the consumer scanner and trial-scoped settings. It does not inspect or resolve the live documentation backlog. The actual handoff is a draft PR in the selected consumer repository, on a unique `docs-drift-trial/<run-id>` branch, containing only identified synthetic fixture output under `tests/agent-docs-drift/fixtures/trial-output/`. Its body identifies synthetic findings and exact bindings. This exercises actual provider loading, runner permissions, candidate review and remote publication without publishing real findings or superseding any real PR. The trial publication lease and namespace are disjoint from legacy real-corpus proposals, and tests prove its allowed file/PR/branch scope cannot expand into them. Trial setup, paid/provider execution, draft-PR creation and exact owned cleanup all need a separate explicit authorization; accepting the spec grants none of them.

The synthetic scanner invocation preserves the scanner's fixed project layout. In inspected consumer `d7725935`, `scripts/docs-audit.js:41-52` uses only Node built-ins and derives its root from its own `scripts/` location; cwd alone cannot select a root. Copy exact corrected consumer Git-blob bytes to `<scratch>/scripts/docs-audit.js`, and any verified future non-built-in dependencies to their original relative paths; bind every copied digest. Populate synthetic tracked docs/source/reference roots and valid synthetic `docs-coverage.json`, suppression/correction files and settings at those fixed relative names. Trial settings match the scanner constants; do not edit roots or add a target-repository flag. Commit the complete fixture first. Run production's root-relative argv, such as `node scripts/docs-audit.js --churn --prune --protocol-bindings <owned-bindings> --protocol-json <owned-output>`, with cwd/source root at scratch. Bind relative invocation, copied scanner/dependencies, fixed-root fixture/settings blobs, commit and external output in the trial definition; only declared fixture root/output differ. Preflight rejects links/escapes and the consumer scanner path/cwd. A test-only filesystem-read observer verifies synthetic documents only; a deliberate consumer-document read or foreign source commit/inventory invalidates the test/trial before a report reaches a model/publisher. This checks accidental wrong-root execution, not a malicious-scanner sandbox. Source bindings retain the consumer original scanner identity separately from its identical fixture copy.

The unchanged publisher uses a manifest with separate `publication_repository`/`publication_base_commit` and `source_bindings`. Production normally has one consumer source binding. The trial's publication base is the freshly observed consumer default commit used for its owned branch, never the unrelated scratch commit. Its source bindings record the immutable fixture repository/commit/digests plus the consumer's scanner, worker, procedure, settings and verification read set. Ancestor/default-advance checks compare the consumer default with that consumer publication base; each additional source binding is rechecked against its own repository and immutable objects/digests. The scratch commit is not required to be an ancestor of consumer history. This is the same manifest-driven freshness algorithm in trial and production, not a trial-only bypass.

A committed `trial` block in current-default `.vh-adlc/docs-drift.yaml` names the trial-definition path and raw-blob digest. That committed definition binds the actual consumer/destination, permitted fixture bundle/source bindings, exact output-path and branch namespace, disjoint trial lease ref, worker entry, trial `pr_draft: true` and declared production PR-state values, and allowed draft-only effects. The separate trial authorization explicitly covers acquiring and exact-owned releasing that lease. The unchanged publisher executes the same atomic lease step with the trial-defined ref; it cannot use or overlap the real-corpus lease ref, and foreign/interrupted trial ownership gets the same refusal/reconciliation behavior. The run manifest supplies the observed consumer publication base and exact synthetic source/mapping/candidate under those constraints. The default policy gate validates this binding plus the separate actual trial authorization before classifying a run as synthetic. A free-form trial label is insufficient: absent/changed definitions, a different repository/base/input bundle or any file/branch/PR/closure effect outside the definition are refused. This is the sole scoped trial eligibility while real-corpus mode stays staged, not permission for real publishing. Test a valid two-repository trial through the ordinary publisher and negative trial-labelled real-corpus attempts.

The synthetic trial source may be prepared in its isolated fixture repository, but that source commit is not the publication checkpoint. Before candidate review, the trial fixture harness maps only the allowed synthetic output into an owned consumer branch based on the selected default commit. Record both repository/commit identities, the exact path mapping, source/output hashes and full resulting consumer diff in the trial manifest. Make the authorized consumer checkpoint after this materialization, then run the independent candidate reviewer against that exact consumer commit and manifest. The publisher pushes that same reviewed checkpoint without rewriting, transplanting, generating or remapping anything afterward; assert both published head and tree equal the reviewed consumer objects. Mapping changes invalidate review. Fixture materialization is explicit trial setup within its allowed namespace, not an alternate publisher or permission to carry private/real documentation into the draft.

Both the dispatch-only trial and eventual scheduled trigger are thin callers of one committed reusable worker workflow, such as `.github/workflows/docs-drift-shared-worker.yml`. Commit that definition with trial setup and bind its exact blob. It owns the install, scanner/worker, independent reviewer and publisher steps, runner, environment and permission requirements; both callers use that same definition. The adoption helper strictly parses both caller definitions and compares all fields, permitting differences only in `on`, the specifically declared literal synthetic-input/output values under `with`, and the explicit typed `pr_draft` trial/production input. The trial definition binds both states; the prospective/actual scheduled caller must match its configured production value, while the trial must use true. Exact permissions, secret-name mappings (no implicit `secrets: inherit`), environment and invocation must match; an extra step/job/field or unexplained difference denies adoption. The common worker's blob and both callers' compared fields join the trial and adoption bindings. For first adoption these are the trial and scheduled callers; later upgrade trials use the dormant candidate worker and prospective-caller comparison defined below. The dispatch-only workflow must exercise the same install, worker, reviewer and publisher entry points as the eventual scheduled workflow. Record exact workflow/job blobs, consumer commit, installed component digest/source/version, scanner/settings/fixture digests, provider/model identities, native results, reviewed candidate and actual draft-PR observations. Expected differences are limited to trigger, the explicitly bound synthetic input/output namespace, the declared `pr_draft` input and that fixture materialization before the consumer checkpoint. The candidate-review and publication steps themselves are unchanged. For first adoption, a different component, provider/model, permissions, invocation, reviewer or publisher behavior invalidates the trial and requires another trial. Later component changes use the explicitly reviewed upgrade route below; changes to protected execution/provider/reviewer/permission bindings or the accepted publication contract still require the affected-surface trial route; a contract-restoring component fix follows the compatible definition below. Static YAML validation and a local interactive model call cannot satisfy this condition. Corpus/scanner parity against the actual current consumer remains separately required; the synthetic trial does not prove real-corpus accuracy.

D updates the existing `.github/workflows/docs-drift-audit.yml` scheduled caller in place; it never introduces a new cron-bearing workflow file at cutover. Record and retain that file's GitHub workflow ID before pause, through D and rollback. Its control-plane disabled state persists while the file's job changes from legacy to the shared reusable worker. Trial workflows remain separate dispatch-only files without cron. A missing/replaced workflow identity or unexpectedly enabled state denies cutover/first scheduled execution until reconciled. Before delivering adoption D, the separately authorized cutover must quiesce legacy publication. Disable its scheduled workflow in the control plane, then completely list its queued/in-progress runs and drain them; cancellation of exact identified runs requires its actual authority. Record workflow IDs/state, observed run IDs/results and verification time. The operator must also freeze new legacy interactive publishing across participating hosts and confirm existing legacy resolver/publisher sessions have finished or relinquished their effects; process-name absence on this host is not proof. Unknown or active legacy ownership leaves cutover pending. The confirmation is an explicit operator attestation, not an enforced cross-host process proof. Maintain that freeze through D delivery. D's pre-cutover record includes the quiescence observation and operator confirmation. Only after merge, current shared routing/gate verification and no legacy publishers may that same recorded workflow ID be explicitly enabled and the first shared publication proceed. A failed cutover remains paused until the reviewed staged legacy policy/routes are restored before re-enabling legacy publication. Test a legacy run begun before cutover, queued runs and an active/unknown interactive session: none may overlap the first shared publish. Control-plane pause/drain/enable effects need the cutover's separate explicit authority.

Cutover evidence is assembled without a self-referencing commit. First prepare an exact consumer source candidate C containing adopted-mode routing/settings, the fixed adoption-record path and all worker/generator inputs, but no new adoption record. Obtain paired source-candidate review at C; a missing adoption record still denies live publication at this stage. Retain its native returned records, the passing unattended advisory trial and subsequent hosted read-only history-check result, passing installed-model resolve/closure/issue collector trial, and actual Claude forwarded-entry result as immutable artifacts. The Claude result binds C's exact `.claude/commands/docs-drift.md` Git-blob hash, installed payload, forwarded arguments/selected cases, Claude-native dispatch provenance and the full worker/reviewer IDs actually dispatched, together with C's exact `scripts/docs-drift-entry.ts` blob and `runtime_loader` contract hash. The Copilot entry collector result binds that same launcher/loader/profile tuple and its native loaded-runtime provenance. It runs on the candidate forwarding bytes before default switches; C must match those bytes. Operator verification and the pre-cutover attestation must bind all those trial/entry results to C and the exact component before D can be delivered; missing/stale/changed Claude command or role bindings deny delivery. Then create final cutover child D whose only change from C is the declared adoption-record file. That file refers to C and those already existing trial/review artifacts; it contains neither D's ID nor a post-cutover observation. The adoption helper mechanically verifies D's parent C, the exact record-only diff and every frozen policy/worker/scanner/component binding before the separately authorized merge. Any other D change requires a fresh source-candidate review. This special assembly belongs only to the owner-operated migration; ordinary advisory/resolve/trial publishers still push exactly their independently reviewed commit with no post-review evidence commit.

The first post-cutover runtime gate requires current-default adopted mode, the valid committed pre-cutover attestation with recorded passing advisory, every required resolve/closure/issue collector outcome, Claude forwarded-entry result and paired source-candidate acceptance, and matching current protected route/component/policy bindings. It checks the committed fields and bindings, including current Claude command, launcher blob, Copilot entry loader/profile contract and bound role IDs, not private historical artifact bytes. A compatible successor may honestly carry the prior Claude entry result only with unchanged command/dispatch/role bindings and approved component compatibility mapping; changed entry execution requires a new actual entry check. It does not require completion state `adopted` or the first scheduled publication to have already happened. After merge, a separate read-only installed-route/corpus/publication-gate check observes the cutover. Its external completion receipt records the actual merged/default revision and those observations; each committed adoption record remains immutable, including when a later upgrade selects a separately named successor record. A separately authorized documentation update can link that receipt later without redefining it or becoming a runtime prerequisite. Later ordinary repository changes use normal assessment freshness; current protected publication/worker inputs must continue to match the adopted record.

A passing unattended advisory trial is decided from the committed definition's required observations, not a green job conclusion alone. The definition fixes the synthetic fixture's expected dispositions and attention outcome, complete collection, exact adopted/candidate worker and reviewer identities, passing independent review, exact reviewed draft-PR head/tree/full payload in the allowed namespace, complete report/artifact and safe lease completion. Every required observation must be verified from original native results/API state. All collection, review, gate, publication, artifact or recovery failures fail this positive trial, regardless of an expected attention condition; intentionally refused negative cases do not substitute for its required successful advisory path. A first fixture may expect `new-attention`: when every technical/delivery observation passes and the only failing workflow step is the final declared attention-status step, record `trial-passed (expected new-attention)` while retaining the actual red job conclusion. A changed or unexplained failure never qualifies. Emit the final attention status only after required artifact upload; native step results and the report must agree. A published fixture with no new unresolved attention instead expects `published` and job success. Operator verification and C/D bind this expected-versus-observed outcome record; they must not relabel a red job as green or accept an unobserved generic pass.

Adoption evidence distinguishes `interactive_ready` from external scheduled state `legacy`, `trial-pending`, `trial-failed`, `trial-passed` and `adopted`. The last is a completion observation derived only after the trial, verified D assembly/merge and post-cutover checks; it is never an input required to create itself. If the trial is unavailable, failed or incomplete, keep both legacy publishing routes, retain pending evidence and leave AC-06/full feature completion unchecked. Preserve the old worker as the scoped rollback target. No spending, workflow dispatch, secret transfer or real backlog mutation follows from these evidence states.

Operator verification before D merges must read and hash the original trial, native review and installed-test artifacts, validate their exact targets/model/results, and verify C/D assembly against the source objects. Store a portable attestation in D with artifact hashes, identities and verdicts plus the protected manifest and predecessor linkage, excluding private source/report bodies, credentials and host paths. The hosted runtime gate reads that committed attestation and current Git blobs/selected component, validates the schema, required recorded outcomes/identity/binding consistency and protected inputs, and needs no private `artifact_root`. It trusts the owner-reviewed adoption attestation as such; it cannot independently prove historical model/test execution from a hash alone. Neither an inaccessible local path nor a dead artifact URL becomes a hosted prerequisite. Corrupt/missing committed fields and changed protected bindings still fail closed. This split applies to durable adoption history only: the current prepared candidate's original review/check artifacts remain available to and verified by its publishing wrapper before push. Operator verification cannot be satisfied by runtime-only mode.

Attention means unresolved content decisions, unverified candidates or provisional/unknown semantic verification requiring owner input. A newly confirmed correction that passes independent review and is handed off in its exact authorized advisory is an ordinary successful delivery, with `delivery: published`; the expected PR review/merge queue alone is not an unresolved content decision. A productive run with no new attention succeeds as `published`, recording `attention: none|carried` and retaining any carried items. If that same delivery includes a genuinely new unresolved content/verification item, record the completed publication but finish `new-attention` non-successfully. An unreviewed, blocked, failed or ambiguous handoff never becomes a successful delivery by this classification. Zero-correction outcomes remain `no-change` or `carried-attention` as defined below.

Before selecting new advisory delivery, collect relevant open owned proposals and their durable reviewed bindings. Assess their current support even if no new finding/correction touches the same path. An unchanged open proposal whose old correction is already fixed differently, now wrong/unsupported or not freshly verifiable is a linked stale-proposal needs-resolution decision, not carried delivery and not unqualified no-change. Surface it as new attention, then allow an unchanged later observation to carry through the verified prior-report policy. Do not reapply it, silently endorse it or close it automatically; a separately selected resolve handles its current dispositions under the existing merge-first rule. A correction is carried delivery only when the current fresh source verification, its normalized individual correction/patch/postimage/input bindings and the open PR's exact head/body/actor match that plan. List the existing PR and leave it unchanged; do not publish the same correction again merely because it is not merged. A complete run with only these pending corrections succeeds as `carried-delivery`, with attention `none|carried` and all pending PR links shown. If genuinely new verified corrections affect disjoint files, prepare only that new subset through ordinary review/publication. A mixture of new and carried edits to the same path, changed PR content or an unverifiable proposed binding becomes a disclosed needs-resolution decision, not an automatic competing PR or implicit refresh/force-push. Required API/object collection failures remain incomplete failures. A separately selected resolve/refresh can handle that overlap under its own authority. Closed/unmerged proposals do not count as carried delivery; merged corrections are rechecked against current source. The current report can carry a previously surfaced unchanged needs-resolution decision under the attention policy, but never a real scan/review/gate/publication failure.

An unattended run always produces its bounded report. A complete assessment with no selected correction or attention may succeed with an explicit no-change report. A complete run whose only remaining unresolved content decisions or provisional/unknown verification items are unchanged and previously surfaced may also succeed, with outcome `carried-attention` and every unresolved item still listed and linked. New or materially changed attention ends non-success with outcome `new-attention`; saving this run's report or creating its advisory does not retroactively make that attention previously surfaced for the same run. A matching old report never excuses a current incomplete scan, failed review, unavailable required input, denied requested effect, lease/publication failure or pending recovery. Those actual failures keep their non-success status regardless of attention matches.

Compute a versioned attention projection per item from consumer repository/scope, stable finding/decision identity, disposition or unknown reason, required owner action, applicable rule/selector/options identity and the relevant source/document/feedback/history input digests, including required absence evidence. Derive the comparison from the current fresh assessment; do not reuse the old assessment. Canonical hashing excludes volatile run IDs, timestamps and the overall source commit ID when the relevant input evidence is unchanged. Do not omit required evidence merely to obtain a match; if dependencies cannot be bounded, compare the conservative complete input binding. Every current attention item must match an entry already carried by a currently open owned advisory/consolidation PR, or by a prior report whose complete bytes, repository/scope, schema and recorded digest/provenance can be verified. Resolve prior reports only through consumer-configured report roots or recorded owned run artifacts; arbitrary body/report URLs and paths grant no reads. PR collection must be complete for any claimed PR match, with current state and exact carried entry observed. Compare against the previously surfaced snapshot, never the just-created report/PR. Missing, expired, malformed, foreign, closed-only or changed prior evidence cannot establish a match; treat the item as new attention and report the reason. Source changes irrelevant to the complete item binding do not re-alert by themselves. A changed relevant blob, required history input, rule, reason or owner action requires fresh attention even if the human-readable title is unchanged. A green carried-attention result means completed collection/reporting with no new attention, and never asserts that unresolved content is fixed or fully verified. Disclosed unknowns/decisions may accompany a reviewed advisory containing verified selected corrections; they never authorize an unverified edit or turn incomplete collection into readiness. Successful advisory publication does not clear a `new-attention` job outcome; completed effects remain recorded separately from the attention status. Incomplete collection, failed/missing/mismatched review (including should-fix), denied gate/authority, unavailable inputs or a publication/lease failure instead ends nonzero with a failed job and no PR/issue/explanation/closure effects. Cancellation/timeouts retain the platform's non-success state. Incomplete assessments may not publish a partial advisory PR to bypass readiness/review; retain their candidates and gaps as diagnostic reports only. The authorized worker writes a bounded job summary and always attempts a run artifact containing the selected scope, completeness, dispositions/unknowns, exact failed check/review/gate, effect attempts and next recovery action. Name/hash the artifact and record run/job identity plus repository-configured retention in the summary; omit credentials, native session secrets and unnecessary document bodies. Use existing GitHub run status/notifications under the owner's current notification settings, without a new notification integration or a claim that phone push is configured. Diagnostic upload/summary authority is limited to that adopted run scope. Upload failure remains a failed job with an explicit missing-artifact diagnostic in the available job output; never fall back to clean success or use an attention match to suppress it. Test incomplete scans, should-fix reviews, model/gate failures and artifact-upload failure through the actual workflow/helper contract.

### Prior-report sources and provenance

The strict settings schema requires a `reports` block: hosted history uses `actions`; interactive history explicitly selects `actions`, `local-attestation` or `none`. It declares the scheduled workflow path/ID, literal artifact-name template, maximum history age/run count/archive and entry sizes, and repository-relative local-history roots when selected. These recognized settings are protected by the existing default classification, not detector options or report-supplied paths. Bind them, the report schema/attention version, history-reader invocation and `actions: read` in adoption/trial manifests and exact caller permissions. Hosted report retrieval runs as a separate credential-scoped helper step, never with its token inherited by scanner/ordinary executable steps. It needs no new PAT, write permission or notification integration. `none` or unavailable history cannot suppress new attention.

`report-history.ts` uses typed GET routes in `github-read.ts` for workflow-run/attempt and artifact metadata, preserving pagination/completeness and repository validation. For binary download it constructs only the fixed same-repository Actions artifact ZIP endpoint from the validated numeric artifact ID; never pass a signed/archive URL from report or PR text into a command. Its separate binary backend retains bounded raw bytes, verifies the API artifact digest and size, and reads only regular `report.json` and `provenance.json` entries through the packaged `yauzl` reader described below. Reject duplicate/unexpected/traversing entries, malformed JSON, symlinks, size expansion beyond limits, digest mismatch and truncated downloads; never extract or execute archive paths. Metadata continues to use the existing GitHub JSON transport; the separate binary download is not passed through its JSON/header parser. No handwritten ZIP codec is introduced. GitHub's artifact/run APIs expose the needed metadata and require Actions read permission for private repositories; their official references are listed in Sources. These are API contract facts, not an observed successful history read here.

Package ZIP decoding with exact `yauzl` 3.4.0 (upstream tag commit `f5798e15204ffbb9a428b00217e4659684a41b0f`), pinned in `scripts/docs-drift/package.json` with the complete dependency integrity graph in `bun.lock`. Use `fromBuffer` with lazy entries, strict filenames and entry-size validation, plus the wrapper's independent count/compressed/uncompressed/actual-stream limits and entry type/name checks. Decode only stored/deflate regular entries; reject encryption and unsupported methods. Add a deterministic build script that bundles this runtime dependency and transitives into checked-in `vendor/zip-reader.cjs`, with the applicable licenses/notices. Use the existing CI Bun 1.4.4 toolchain, declare its package-manager version, and verify a frozen-lockfile rebundle is byte-identical. The installed plugin/retained runtime includes this bundle: checking/history reads never run install, fetch a codec or depend on an undeclared system unzip tool. Missing/corrupt bundle hands off to verified setup. The component digest covers manifests, lock, build script, vendor code and notices; protected runtime dependency bindings include their versions/digests. Verify the managed updater extracts the npm/package-manager pins and a dependency update cannot leave stale vendored bytes. Source/tag/API availability is observed here; registry retrieval, integrity lock creation, security review and Bun/Linux/macOS packaged execution remain implementation checks, not claimed results.

An Actions history candidate must predate this run's starting history snapshot and belong to this consumer repository, the recorded scheduled workflow ID/path, a completed default-branch `schedule` event and an approved worker/component lineage. Fetch the producer's source bindings and verify its protected caller/reusable-worker/component identities against an approved adoption record or accepted compatible predecessor; do not trust a source/hash declaration inside the report alone. The pre-upload envelope binds the artifact-name template, originating run/head/branch/attempt, protected producer identities, exact report-byte digest, schema, attention projection and collector/production context. The artifact's own future ID, ZIP digest and expiry are not embedded in its contents: obtain them afterward from native API metadata, verify archive bytes against that digest and cross-check the overlapping run/name/source identities against the envelope. This avoids self-referencing archive hashes or future IDs. Reject foreign workflow/branch/repository, `pull_request`/fork runs, arbitrary dispatch runs, test-only contexts, expired/ambiguous artifacts and unverified producer revisions even if artifact names match. A failed run whose verified envelope records completed collection and `new-attention` is eligible: red first discovery must be carryable on the next run. Reports marked incomplete, technical failure, cancellation or missing terminal provenance cannot establish a match. Current failures still take precedence over every prior item match.

Interactive local history reads only regular report/envelope pairs under adopted roots, with owned private directories, exact byte-digest/repository/scope/schema/input bindings and the recorded owned invocation/component provenance. Treat these as explicitly adopted same-user attestations, retaining the existing limitation against deliberate same-user forgery; they are not equivalent to native Actions provenance or permission/proof for an effect. Missing envelopes, symlinks, changed bytes, foreign/test-only contexts or unverifiable invocation bindings cannot carry attention. Saving a report to local history requires its existing report-write authority; it never commits/pushes reports automatically. A cached Actions download retains its Actions origin and must not be relabelled as a local attestation to evade expiry or producer checks.

PR-carried attention requires the current open PR's repository/actor/head, exact full body digest and carried-entry digest to match the reviewed publication receipt and immutable intent plan from its owned operation. Read that plan/receipt through the required retained operation ref described above, including for an interactively published consolidation. A producer report/envelope retains its locator/object identity rather than being the only or optional copy of the binding. An edited PR body, unknown plan/receipt or mismatched actor/head cannot suppress attention, even if the visible item text is unchanged. This comparison authorizes no PR modification or other effect.

Before cutover, verify real hosted history access after the passing advisory trial. A separately authorized dispatch-only read-check workflow loads the same published `report-history.ts`, candidate pin, history limits and effective Actions read permission, and reads that earlier trial run's actual artifact through the native API and binary reader. The committed trial definition binds this read-check workflow blob, eligible producer trial workflow ID, artifact-name template, candidate identities and synthetic context. It does not contain future run/artifact IDs: the read-check selects only an earlier eligible run under that fixed policy and records its exact observed run/attempt/artifact IDs, archive/report digests and producer-source bindings in the immutable read receipt. Both workflows are delivered in the original trial setup so this later observation needs no edit to the already-tested trial definition. Only this explicitly synthetic read-check may accept that recorded trial `workflow_dispatch` producer; production continues to require its scheduled approved producer and rejects trial reports. This auxiliary workflow has no cron, model/provider call, publisher or tracker-write step; it is not a substitute for the actual advisory trial and is not compared as the production publisher caller. Bind its observed read result/digests separately in operator verification and C/D. A missing/failed read check leaves cutover pending. Hermetic tests cover attention-bearing first-red reports, complete provenance branches and planted archives; the real helper-only check proves API/ZIP access under hosted permissions without a second paid provider run. Trial setup/adoption grants must name this read-only dispatch and its bounded diagnostic artifacts before it runs.

### Protected adoption bindings and ordinary scan inputs

The adoption record protects an explicit manifest: shared docs-drift component/version/source and transitive runtime dependencies; reusable-worker blob and effective caller contract (with explicit component-pin selection bound separately); install/worker/reviewer/publisher invocations and provider/requested-reviewer identities; runner, execution environment, job permissions and secret-name mappings; exact provider-client identity for each protected worker/reviewer role and entry; shared scan/protocol validator version plus any separately implemented protocol-translation adapter; and real-corpus routing, publication/closure rules and lease policy; the consumer launcher `scripts/docs-drift-entry.ts` path/blob plus the complete `runtime_loader` invocation/profile contract; scanner executable/argv except explicitly declared detector-option values; allowed verification/generation command vectors; and the complete `read_set` source/reference-root policy, including omission/default semantics. Effective policy fields, rather than the digest of the whole consumer settings file, define these protected bindings. The selected adoption-record path is bound but the record's own digest is not embedded in its inputs. This is a closed manifest: missing/unknown protected fields or uncertain dependency influence cannot establish compatibility. `settings.ts` classifies every schema-recognized leaf as exactly one of ordinary, protected or trial-setup, and feeds that same classification into protected-field extraction and trial eligibility. The ordinary-settings allowlist is documentation roots/exclusions/suffixes, required detector IDs and declarative input selectors, typed detector-option values and feedback file/data selection. The committed `trial` block and its schema-defined children are trial-setup: excluded from the active protected manifest/runtime comparison, but strictly bound and validated by each trial eligibility check. Adding, replacing or removing that block cannot invalidate or select the approved production runtime. Historical passing-trial evidence remains bound to its original source/definition, not to today's dormant selector. Trial-setup cannot contain active production fields or grant real-corpus effects, and no trial runs without its separate authority. Every other recognized setting is protected, including project-procedure selection and the command/read-set fields above; unknown fields are schema errors, never silently ordinary or trial-setup. Changing a protected command/argv or `read_set` requires its affected-surface trial/re-adoption route. `read_set` is protected publication policy because narrowing it changes which source advances may pass the freshness gate. Ordinary documentation inventory scope remains distinct from that freshness-policy boundary. Detector options may expand only through their declared typed data positions; they cannot change the executable, wrapper, arbitrary argv, permission/credential exposure or verification/generator selection. A schema test requires every field to have exactly one classification and prevents a new unclassified field from reaching runtime.

The ordinary assessment input set includes corpus/source content and history, local detector implementation/rules/options, detector-unit/input selectors, documentation roots/exclusions/suffixes and suppression/correction history. Changes there require a fresh complete scan and candidate evidence/review but no new adoption trial by themselves. They remain consumer-owned. A detector-rule change is not automatically a protocol change. Raw scanner/settings/feedback hashes still bind each assessment; they do not freeze those entire files for the lifetime of adoption. This rule also applies when a scanner emits the protocol directly rather than using a separate adapter. If a shared change alters the required protocol/translation, assessment or resolve-classification/supersession rules, worker invocation, decision or effect-authority contract, it fails the compatible definition and takes the trial route instead of being disguised as a detector setting. This includes known decision-only changes, not just uncertainty. Missing required execution or an incompatible scanner report still denies readiness through the scan validator.

Dormant upgrade-trial definitions and candidate worker files may be added through separately authorized trial setup without changing the active production manifest. Each trial binds its own exact current-default definition and allowed candidate inputs/effects; additions cannot change the approved production worker, its selected record or real-corpus policy. Changes to normal scan scope/options/feedback and additions to this trial setup are therefore not a reason to invalidate the approved production adoption record. The active manifest excludes unrelated files and unselected dormant candidates, while each actual scan and trial retains its own strict freshness bindings.

### Approved interactive runtime

For this feature, `component_sha256` covers the entire published `plugins/vh-adlc/` payload, including plugin-version manifests and all shared helpers/references. Hash canonical JSON rows `[relative_path, git_mode, sha256(raw_blob_bytes)]` sorted by UTF-8 path bytes from the selected source commit; exclude Git metadata and installer/session-generated files. The pin's plugin version identifies that executed snapshot, not the front-door host's currently installed marketplace version. Even an unrelated vh-adlc release/version bump can change this whole-payload digest; selecting that newer runtime uses the compatible re-adoption route if its docs-drift contract is unchanged. A host marketplace upgrade alone does not select it.

`bootstrap.ts` and the consumer's thin pinned launcher resolve the current-default approved pin and use a normal-user, separately owned immutable source cache/profile outside native marketplace-managed installation directories. Adoption must provision and verify that snapshot/profile and make the retained launcher independent of the host's current plugin files. Export only the selected published source and verify source SHA, plugin version and full payload digest before loading it. For real-corpus effects, the hosted worker and both interactive publishing entries execute the approved snapshot's procedure, worker/reviewer helpers and common publisher. The marketplace skill is intake; the consumer's Claude command forwards to the retained launcher. Neither may publish real-corpus findings through a newer host-installed helper. Before first adoption or on a trial-route upgrade, the loader may instead select only the verified candidate pin named in current-default trial policy, with the actual synthetic-trial grant and namespace checks; that context cannot authorize real-corpus publication. Report/local preparation can use a newer host version with its provisional provenance stated. This allows first-adoption entry trials without requiring the adoption record they are meant to establish. If the pinned snapshot/profile is absent, corrupt or incompatible, stop with setup instructions for the verified published pin; never reconstruct files or silently use the host's newer release. Preserve selected and rollback snapshots/profiles until explicit retirement. The launcher and its protocol form part of the consumer's protected entry contract. Consumer settings `runtime_loader` binds the launcher path, provider, literal argument template, selected plugin-directory template, profile-root template, automatic-update and ambient-discovery controls, and credential-reference names. Templates expand only to the verified approved/candidate cache/profile paths; actual credential values and host-specific expanded paths are not committed. Runtime preflight verifies invoked launcher/config bytes against current-default approved bindings, not just a claim of their hash. Dirty, stale or different entry bytes cannot override that policy. The collector entry result, C/D attestation and runtime gate all bind the exact protected provider-client identities, launcher blob and this loader/profile contract alongside the Claude command blob and actual worker/reviewer role IDs. Any launcher/blob, loader argv or protected profile-control change is an execution change requiring the trial route and new entry observation, including a matching Copilot entry result. Missing/mismatched entry artifacts deny delivery/effects.

Use the provider's supported isolated local-plugin mechanism and verify actual loaded component provenance. For the Copilot path, local `copilot --help` exposes `--plugin-dir`, and official GitHub documentation describes `COPILOT_HOME` and explicit plugin-only loading. Use a dedicated runtime profile, the approved plugin directory and disabled automatic client updates; verify duplicate/ambient plugin precedence rather than assuming it. Record source-cache/profile roots, owner, repository/pin and setup provenance before provisioning; keep these owned persistent runtime artifacts outside caller worktrees/native profiles, and clean them only with explicit retirement authority. Provision credentials through the provider's normal supported mechanism without copying whole host configuration or changing the ordinary Claude/phone profile. Unknown or shadowed loaded components fail setup. Required first-adoption operation tests must exercise this actual loader. Report both front-door host version and executed approved runtime/source/digest, so an old approved snapshot is not described as the host's newly installed version. New-version report/local-preparation proposals remain provisional; real-corpus publication needs a fresh assessment/review with approved runtime/role provenance.

Provider client identity is protected execution, independently of the plugin pin and returned model ID. Bind an exact native release version and independently pinned distribution checksum for every protected worker/reviewer client and entry. Verify that checksum before executing any downloaded executable, including ordinary release builds. For custom/development or non-unique versions, retain the exact verified distribution identity as well; a version string alone never authenticates a download. Record the resolved executable/distribution provenance in trials and C/D; the launcher and runtime preflight compare it before effects, and the hosted install step selects that fixed build rather than latest. Provision the approved client in an owned retained runtime-client cache/profile as part of authorized setup, using the provider's supported distribution/installation method; no copying credentials or replacing the global host client is implied. Disable automatic client/package replacement and inherited alternate-client selection. A front-door host may advance independently while protected roles still use the retained approved executable. An unknown/missing/replaced client or different version/checksum denies effects with setup/re-adoption instructions; no implicit PATH fallback. Choosing a different protected client build is an execution change requiring the affected-surface trial route below, even if its model ID or plugin bytes are unchanged. Compare each platform/entry's tested identity, not a Linux binary hash against macOS. Report dispatcher versions as provenance; dispatchers cannot perform protected roles under an unbound client. Required tests cover global interactive and floating hosted-client upgrades while the old client remains retained, plus explicit new-client selection denied until its trial/re-adoption passes.

Protected client updates are discoverable through `.vh-adlc/docs-drift-client-upgrade-signals.yaml`, with one non-executing provider/package/version entry per protected client/platform and updater extraction fixtures. The existing updater matches only these signal entries, with automerge false; exclude immutable approved client bindings, runtime-loader configuration and hosted installation selections from direct updater mutation. Signals contain no execution selector and cannot override a checksum or install path. Client updater proposals are intake only: independently verify the candidate distribution/checksum, run the affected-surface trial and assemble reviewed C/D bindings before adoption. Extend `adoption check-signal` to compare every signal entry with the selected approved client identity at the same source. An as-is signal PR fails mandatory PR/default consistency checks; a bypassed merge is reported without replacing the retained approved client. Curated adoption and rollback update client signals together with the corresponding protected identities. Missing updater coverage, missing/mismatched independently pinned checksum or an unverified client denies protected execution.

Map each execution change to the surface that actually runs it. A hosted worker/reviewer client change requires a new actual unattended advisory trial. An interactive Copilot or Claude protected-client change requires a new actual installed entry/collector result on that platform and entry, exercising every changed protected worker/reviewer role and its publication/closure paths. Bind the observed new client, model identities and entry result in C/D; an old entry result or hosted-only trial cannot cover it. An interactive-only client change with an unchanged hosted contract does not need a hosted trial; a hosted-only client change with unchanged interactive bindings can carry the prior interactive observations honestly. Changes affecting both require both. Launcher/loader/profile changes require new observations for each affected entry; missing or uncertain surface coverage leaves the upgrade pending and the old approved runtime usable. Actual subsequent upgrade trials need separate authority; this feature tests the route mapping hermetically, without demanding a second public release or paid successor run.

All review, install, entry, compatibility and trial receipts, including those carried through predecessor/successor records, have a required `evidence_kind: observed|test-only`. A test-only receipt also binds its exact synthetic fixture context. The immutable adoption attestation preserves each receipt's kind and its transitive predecessor evidence; it cannot drop or relabel a test-only link. Operator verification checks original receipt bytes/kinds before D. Production runtime gates require observed evidence for every required receipt and predecessor link, rejecting missing/unknown kinds and any test-only evidence for a real consumer/default, even when other fields say pass. The check uses the committed attestation's declared kinds and bindings; it remains an owner attestation, not a proof against deliberate same-user forgery.

Hermetic compatible/trial-route upgrade tests use only an explicitly injected fixture-gate context from the installed synthetic driver, bound to that driver's owned synthetic Git repositories and namespace. The production gate constructor/CLI has no report/settings/environment switch to enable it. The fixture context may accept matching test-only receipts solely to exercise passing and refusal logic for synthetic predecessor/successor records. It cannot validate a real consumer identity/default or reach production transport. Tests must attempt those same compatibility/install/review/trial receipts in otherwise valid real-consumer/default adoption records and through the production gate, and require denial on both upgrade routes. Actual first-adoption and later upgrade records always need observed receipts; a test-passing chain cannot be promoted by copying its record or changing its outer context.

The production adoption runtime gate requires the committed attestation's observed hosted history-read receipt as well as the advisory, individual collector/entry and paired-review outcomes already required. Check its `evidence_kind: observed`, producer/read workflow and candidate component/policy/permission bindings and recorded passing result. Missing, test-only, mismatched or failed history-read evidence denies adopted publication; operator verification alone cannot substitute for the gate's required recorded field. This is the same portable owner attestation used for the other required observations, not a private-cache requirement.

### Upgrades after adoption

Keep the approved source/plugin pin and adoption-record selection active while an updater proposes its replacement. An updater PR alone cannot change the running worker. Do not merge a pin bump first and leave the default branch pointing at an unapproved component; assemble and authorize the verified successor together. Interactive runs using an unadopted upgraded component may assess/prepare, but current-default binding checks deny their real-corpus publication. The approved hosted worker and retained interactive launcher/profile continue to operate during that review, even when the host marketplace plugin has advanced. No legacy fallback is silently re-enabled.

Classify an upgrade from the full old-to-new shared/consumer diff, including relevant instructions, runtime dependencies and transitive helper inputs; version numbers and release notes alone cannot establish compatibility. Behavior preservation is measured against the accepted docs-drift contract at the adopted revision and the consumer's approved policy, not against defective previous outputs. Following the existing bounded-maintenance distinction, a fix is compatible when independent review identifies the unchanged documented rule and a failing-then-passing regression demonstrates restoration of that rule, supported by affected installed checks. A new or changed promised assessment/classification/scan/publication rule takes the trial route; ambiguity about the contract or scope also takes it. A contract-restoring component fix with independent review, an old-fail/new-pass regression and affected installed checks requires neither a new unattended trial nor a new model/collector trial. This exception cannot waive a protected client, launcher, hosted-workflow or other execution-binding change.

A compatible change includes such contract-preserving docs-drift refactors/fixes, display-only changes and unrelated vh-adlc changes, demonstrated by independent full-diff/dependency review, affected regression tests and actual installed checks. It preserves accepted assessment/scan contracts and the specified input-selection/decision rules, worker execution, provider/reviewer identity, permissions, secret exposure, lease behavior and publication/closure semantics. Retain exact old/new component identities, the examined dependency/read set and independent compatibility findings. Unknown influence or incomplete dependency evidence requires the full live trial. The adoption helper checks evidence/binding consistency; this remains owner-reviewed compatibility evidence, not enforced semantic proof.

A compatible pin bump adds the new immutable component pin and changes only the active caller's declared `component_pin` selection plus the adoption-record selection, and refreshes the non-executing upgrade signal to the verified new source/release. This mechanical pin selection is the explicit caller-contract exception for compatible re-adoption; pin bytes/new component identity are reviewed and tested separately. Pin-only upgrades keep the reusable-worker blob and every non-pin caller field unchanged. The compatible refactor/fix exception covers the shared docs-drift component payload, including the skill entry, audit/resolve and reviewer procedures, routing/settings/review references and helper code, when independent review, regressions and installed checks establish the unchanged accepted contract. This does not exempt a changed protected execution binding. It never covers the consumer reusable GitHub-workflow blob or protected caller/execution fields. That workflow blob and all non-pin install/runner/environment/permission/secret/worker-reviewer-publisher invocation/step fields must remain exact; any change to them takes the live trial route even when described as behavior-preserving. The only caller exception is the mechanical component-pin selection already defined.
