The compatible successor binds new component-internal hashes and their independently reviewed old-to-new contract mapping, without pretending the old trial ran those bytes. Changes to the accepted decision rules/contracts/effect authority, or uncertainty, take the full trial route. A candidate trial and its prospective scheduled caller select the same candidate pin, so their comparison does not ignore arbitrary extra inputs.

For a compatible upgrade, obtain independent review and actual fresh/upgrade installed-payload checks for the new exact version, plus affected regressions and consumer command/worker contract checks. Prepare source candidate C with the new component pin and a distinct successor-record path while the approved default stays unchanged. Obtain paired source/compatibility acceptance at C, then use the same record-only child D assembly and separately authorized delivery as first adoption. The successor record binds the previous immutable adoption record and its passing trial, exact old/new components, compatibility classification, installed-test artifacts and fresh C review. The gate accepts this explicit compatible-re-adoption chain only when invariant behavior/policy bindings match, changed component-internal hashes have the independently approved compatibility mapping and installed/regression evidence, unchanged hosted-workflow/non-pin execution bindings match the predecessor, and the current component equals the new reviewed/tested value. It does not pretend the old trial executed the new bytes, or require those bytes to equal the old trial's component. A missing/stale compatibility review or installed check denies the upgrade. Keep prior records; update the selected record path rather than overwriting evidence.

Every shared change that fails the compatible-upgrade definition requires the affected-surface trials defined above, followed by C review, record-only D assembly and post-update observations. A shared change to hosted-worker rules/helpers that fails the compatible definition requires another separately authorized live unattended trial at the proposed version. An interactive-only protected-client or entry change follows its actual installed-entry trial route; a hosted trial cannot substitute for it. This includes changes to accepted assessment, resolve-classification/supersession, scan-contract, execution, provider/reviewer, permission/secret, lease or publication/closure rules/behavior, rather than restoration of an unchanged accepted contract; uncertainty also takes this trial route. No known non-compatible change can fall between the two routes. Local detector rules, ordinary scan scope/options and feedback remain normal assessment inputs as listed above. To run that upgrade trial while production remains approved, separately authorize a default-branch setup that adds a distinct dormant candidate reusable-worker file and a dispatch-only caller. The current-default trial definition binds their exact blobs, proposed component/provider/permissions, allowed synthetic namespace and the intended prospective scheduled-caller contract. The active scheduled caller still references the old approved worker and record; it does not share the candidate file yet. The bounded synthetic trial may load only that explicitly named candidate, even though it differs from the active production binding, and cannot publish real-corpus findings. During this interval compare the trial caller to the prospective scheduled-caller contract, not the still-active old caller. After the trial, source candidate C switches the scheduled caller to that exact tested candidate worker; mechanically require its normalized caller fields and all protected candidate bindings to match the passing trial. D adds only the new immutable record. An unexpected candidate ref/blob, permission, invocation or caller difference requires re-review/retrial. For this feature's upgrade-procedure proof, hermetically simulate a permission- or execution-changing candidate trial with synthetic Git repositories/pins/callers and authenticated-as-test receipts; verify the old approved worker gate remains usable. This is not a live successor trial claim. The same conservative path applies when compatibility is uncertain. A failed/unavailable upgrade trial leaves the approved default pin/record active. Neither classification grants automatic merge, publication, paid execution or secret transfer. Every real adopted upgrade route refreshes the non-executing upgrade signal to the verified new source/release in C. After an authorized upgrade, recheck installed routing and gate behavior at the observed default. Rollback selects the prior approved pin, record, its corresponding source/release signal and matching migration-owned inputs together; preserve unrelated newer work and refuse mismatched combinations. A reverted signal may produce another intake proposal for the rolled-back release; that proposal cannot auto-merge or execute and needs fresh selection/verification. Do not rewrite historical pins or silently add updater-ignore policy during rollback.

An assessment-rule, scan-contract, scheduled-status, attention-projection/carry or report-history/provenance trial-route upgrade must bind a changed-rule coverage map in its committed trial definition. For each changed contract, name a bounded fixture scenario, exact fixture bytes and old/new contract references, with discriminatory expected dispositions or protocol outcomes under those two rules. Independently review that mapping; an unrelated unchanged first-adoption fixture cannot prove the new rule. The actual candidate trial must observe every mapped scenario and satisfy the new expected outcome through its real assessment/scan path. Missing coverage, a skipped case or a generic passing job without those observations denies upgrade verification. The old expectation can be grounded in the old accepted contract and hermetic old-component regression where applicable; this adds no implicit paid old-provider rerun. Hermetic upgrade-procedure tests use matching explicitly test-only observations, retaining their production rejection. Actual adopted successors require the corresponding observed candidate-trial evidence. Status/attention/history changes, protected report settings and history permission changes also require a candidate hosted read check: retrieve an actual earlier eligible trial artifact under the proposed Actions permission and report policy, then exercise the reviewed old/new projection/eligibility rules against unchanged-carried and changed/new-attention fixture cases. The helper check executes the candidate status/history code against bound assessment fixtures without a second paid provider call; retain that synthetic-assessment limitation. An advisory-only run or a binary download without those case observations cannot verify this surface. Candidate policies/workflows live only in the separately authorized default-branch trial setup while the old production settings/pin stay active; C selects exactly the tested prospective policy, and D records the observed check.

First adoption and the upgrade verification suite must exercise both supported surfaces. Before first cutover, and again when the accepted resolve-classification/supersession, PR/issue publication or closure contract changes, run a bounded actual model resolve trial using the exact installed candidate and synthetic proposal/diff/feedback/merge histories through a hermetic remote collector. It must observe consolidation publication, denial before merge, verified post-merge explanation/closure, abandonment/revert refusal, and exact scoped issue creation with missing/stale-review, wrong-model, absent-grant and staged-gate refusals; assert the exact attempted effects and that no real backlog IDs can enter the namespace. The actual model executes the installed resolve procedure; the tracker transport and merge event are fixtures, so report this as a model/collector trial rather than a real GitHub merge/closure. Require individually passing resolve, closure and issue collector cases in the first-adoption attestation before D; an advisory-only or generic collector-pass flag cannot substitute. Issue-affecting trial-route upgrades must pass that issue case again at the new exact payload. For a change affecting the hosted contract, this supplements its separately authorized unattended advisory trial; advisory success alone cannot verify a changed resolver. An interactive-only client change follows the affected-entry mapping above. Lease/advisory changes must likewise exercise the corresponding contention/publication path. A missing affected-surface trial leaves the upgrade unverified even when its other trial passes.

The AC-06 proofs for later upgrade/re-adoption/rollback machinery are hermetic helper/runtime-gate scenarios using synthetic version pins, workflow definitions, Git defaults, predecessor/successor records and clearly test-only trial/install receipts. They need no second published release, second paid hosted trial or candidate setup on Stackweaver default during first adoption. When a real later upgrade is selected, its actual new-version installed checks and applicable live trials are required by the operational procedure above; fixture receipts cannot satisfy them. The actual first-release marketplace fresh installs/upgrades and first-adoption advisory/resolve/closure/issue trials remain mandatory and are not replaced by these hermetic upgrade tests.

### Migration and rollback

Before retiring either local route, inventory every caller, command, scheduled worker, scanner option, feedback reader and generated-output dependency. Record old-to-new behavior and explicit intentional differences. Keep the scanner, suppressions, correction history, docs-truth workflow and local project policy in Stackweaver; preserve their bytes except the owner-selected bounded collection-accounting correction and the reviewed routing/configuration migration. Detector rules and existing feedback contents remain unchanged.

Publish the shared feature only under separate authority, then verify actual native Codex, Claude and Copilot marketplace fresh installs/upgrades, discovery and installed execution. Copilot uses the existing repository-root `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` catalog; Codex uses `.agents/plugins/marketplace.json`. Verify that source choice during native installation rather than adding an unverified parallel catalog. For Copilot, register the published `michielvha/vh-adlc` marketplace with `copilot plugin marketplace add`, install `vh-adlc@vhco-tools` using `copilot plugin install` in an isolated `COPILOT_HOME`, and observe native plugin listing plus discovery/loading of the named `vh-adlc-docs-drift` skill. Record the catalog source/revision, front-door plugin version/payload, native discovery artifact and actual invocation. Upgrade a prior published install in a separate isolated profile and check the resulting payload/discovery again. Local copies or `--plugin-dir` alone do not satisfy this front-door marketplace proof; `--plugin-dir` remains the explicit retained approved-runtime mechanism. Document the commands in the installation/marketplace guides and keep the Copilot publishing entry unclaimed and cutover denied if that actual source/install/discovery fails. Local `copilot plugin`, `plugin install`, `plugin marketplace add` and `plugin list --help` confirm these native command forms; this is command-availability evidence, not a successful install. Use bounded synthetic source/docs/PR/feedback scenarios for actual model trials; do not resolve real drift PRs or publish real findings during acceptance. GUI/phone checks remain unclaimed without observed coverage or a separately approved visible deferral. The real unattended worker trial is required before scheduled cutover under the staged choice above; a generic deferral cannot silently replace that requirement.

Fresh paired review binds the exact installed shared payload and consumer candidate. Complete corpus/legacy behavior checks and conflict-safe rollback/reapply must pass before consumer merge. Rollback restores migration-owned routing/configuration including the publication gate to staged mode before the legacy publisher resumes, and preserves scanner/feedback/customer documents, other workflows and newer unrelated changes. After merge, recheck installed routing and the local scheduled-worker contract at the observed revision. Do not label static YAML validation as a live scheduled provider run.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-01: An adopting repository can assess whole-corpus documentation drift through one discoverable harness-agnostic workflow while retaining its scanner, detector policy and existing change-scoped docs-impact behavior.
- [ ] AC-02: Assessments expose incomplete collection, unknown findings and stale inputs without claiming a clean or fully verified corpus, and ground proposed fixes in the selected source and document evidence. Collection is complete only when the scanner's reported document and per-unit inventories and digests match the expected sets/digests the shared helper independently derives from the bound Git tree and committed selectors, with successful required-unit outcomes and no failed, skipped or partial required collection. A scanner report or echoed digest without that match cannot establish completeness. This cross-checks reported input coverage; it does not prove detector interpretation or resist deliberate evidence forgery.
- [ ] AC-03: Authorized preparation and resolution preserve unrelated work, intended contracts, historical documents and local feature-review rules while producing only the verified selected corrections and decisions.
- [ ] AC-04: Rejected proposals produce the correct detector-versus-interpretation feedback, and duplicate or conflicting stale proposals cannot silently reintroduce rejected or unsupported changes. An original proposal closes only after its merged consolidation is freshly verified to carry every hunk or its terminal disposition; otherwise the original stays open.
- [ ] AC-05: Reports let an owner understand the result and decisions without reading the full audit, and independent review precedes publication, and all local and remote effects retain their declared scope and permission boundaries. Every real-corpus PR publishes the exact independently reviewed candidate commit/tree. Each real-corpus PR, issue, explanation and closure effect requires current independent review of its exact payload and bound source with the adopted reviewer identity; changed candidates or payloads cannot reuse the old review. Interrupted effects reconcile against owned intents and exact remote observations without duplicate PRs, issues, comments or closures; ambiguous or foreign state remains pending instead of being replayed. A complete scheduled run with only unchanged previously surfaced attention succeeds while retaining all unresolved items in its report; new attention and actual incomplete, review, gate, publication or recovery failures remain non-successful. A complete scheduled run whose confirmed corrections are all unchanged carried deliveries in verified open owned PRs succeeds without a duplicate PR or head rewrite and still lists those pending PRs. An explicitly approved delivery acknowledgement requires unique direct application evidence, original run ownership, exact current target bindings and settled mutation requests. It preserves the changed resource, durably stops original business replay and permits only exact-owned cleanup after all effects are accounted for, retaining a non-success result.
- [ ] AC-06: Stackweaver uses the published installed workflow with verified legacy command compatibility and staged scheduled-worker cutover only after an observed passing unattended shared trial, with the legacy scheduled worker retained until that success, retained local scanner/feedback/site rules and a conflict-safe rollback path. After adoption, its approved pin and record remain active until a successor is approved through independent review and installed checks for compatible changes, or the required affected-surface trials for other changes; an update proposal cannot switch runtime execution. Before any trial or shared interactive adoption, Stackweaver has the delivered scanner-completeness correction: its separate protocol reports skipped/failed reads and required-unit execution while legacy report fields, detector results and exits remain unchanged.
- [ ] AC-07: Required repository and portable-host checks pass at recorded revisions. Actual published-marketplace fresh installs/upgrades and named-skill discovery pass for Codex, Claude and Copilot; actual bounded Copilot model operations/reviews and Claude daily-command forwarding through bound roles pass through their declared synthetic transports. Paired Stackweaver migration and rollback checks pass at the exact shared/consumer payloads before cutover. Unperformed model, GUI, phone or other surfaces remain explicitly unclaimed; installer/helper results alone cannot establish operation support.

## Test Plan

| Criterion | Proof |
| --- | --- |
| AC-01 | Install/discover the content operation and run it in a second synthetic adopter with different roots, statuses, scanner and generated files. Assert the documented request examples choose audit/resolve/docs-impact/plain-doc/persistent-audit/truth routes correctly; ambiguous wording asks once. Test missing settings, unpinned setup, missing knowledge and unchanged existing operations. Load every shipped docs-drift example through the strict settings validator and exercise its scan contract with the matching fixture, including `detector_inputs`, publication/trial policy and required reviewer identity; example/interface drift fails the check. |
| AC-02 | Exercise complete empty scans, findings, missing/malformed/stale reports, skipped or unreadable inputs, unknown detectors, suppressed findings and stale suppressions. Assert each incomplete source remains visible and prevents clean/ready claims. Independently enumerate the exact configured Git-tree corpus and compare every read/digest/failure entry; test omissions, duplicates, escapes, symlinks, all-docs-read/missing-detector and throwing-detector cases against the configured required unit set. Derive each unit's expected document set from committed selectors; deny a complete unit that examines only a strict subset, and test legitimate all-inventory, subset and zero-input selectors. Check the exact v1 preimage/canonical ordering in helper-generated binding JSON, scanner echo/actual accounting and helper recomputation, plus independent raw-blob digest agreement, including mixed-case/non-ASCII option keys, literal backslashes, invalid encodings and checkout filters. Test inherited GIT_DIR/GIT_INDEX_FILE/object/config overrides, replace refs on document blobs and missing promisor objects: return unaltered bound bytes or incomplete, with no lazy fetch, wrong-index read or caller-state mutation. Cover the scanner child environment and owned fetch path as well as direct helper reads. Test optional source/reference roots in scope hashing, whole-repository fallback and negative searches; scanner feedback/tree/index/ref/FETCH_HEAD mutation must make the assessment incomplete. Bind scans, evidence and rechecks to exact source/options; include source changes during assessment and malicious report/doc instructions. Run the scanner with canary provider/GitHub/mapped secrets in the parent and an ambient Git credential helper/extraheader: the child sees no tokens, helper or agent socket, and a credentialed push attempt cannot produce an authenticated effect. Verify no persisted checkout credentials and no secret-bearing job environment reach collection; inaccessible required inputs yield incomplete assessment. These tests prove ambient separation, not malicious same-user filesystem confinement. |
| AC-03 | Verify report-only preservation of files/index/refs; owned prepare/resolve edits; unrelated staged/unstaged work; historical/other-system/intended-contract and deliberately incomplete matrix cases; code-bug routing; uncertain/runtime acceptance boxes and local-format plan gates. Require explicit selection for destructive/structural and plan/status decisions. Exercise committed `resolveRoots`, shared strict metadata reading and the existing matcher's feature dialect against actual Go MatchCovers shared fixtures. Require the same expected matches for all supported single-star and double-star patterns, including every current vector in `tests/vh-adlc/loop/covers-cases.json`. Verify root, nested, directory-suffix, prefix-boundary, leading-dot, escape, Unicode and newline cases and malformed patterns. Include `a[/]**` and `a[^x]**` against `a/b`, `**/*??` against a single multibyte rune, `**/?.md` against a multibyte-rune filename, `src/\**`, and `src/**/` routed before directory-suffix matching. The ordinary protection test first observes `covers: [docs/**]` causing a classification gap. After parity, it identifies the affected nested guide and enforces active-feature coordination or records historical coverage. Verify the existing docs-impact default behavior and local-format cases. Actual freshness uses the binary. Standard Review Log text, Evidence/Residual blocks, Task proof/completion history and dated Context/Sources provenance must remain byte-identical, including a stale link inside a stored review finding and inside an Evidence line; propose corrections through the feature owner instead. Update the docs-impact isolated-plugin copy list in `tests/vh-adlc/docs-impact/cli.test.ts` for `scripts/git-read.ts` and verify unchanged candidates/gaps for metadata it previously accepted. Standard normative sections/covers and every part of claimed feature documents must remain byte-identical and route to the spec/claiming-writer handoff. A guide covered by an active claimed feature is proposal-only without an actual handoff/coordinated owner selection; report affected features and freshness changes for ordinary covered documents including historical completed coverage. Missing independent verification remains provisional. Verify recorded assessment-role PR object stores and trial scratch repositories remain outside caller Git directories and can never change caller objects/refs/FETCH_HEAD/index or clean a foreign resource. With local feature-document format, put a stale path inside a recorded review/evidence range that the retained procedure does not classify. Preparation/resolution must report the correction and preserve the plan byte-for-byte; only explicitly editable local ranges may change. A custom-root AGENTS.md that is unreadable/malformed, or a missing HEAD, must leave every corpus document untouched and assessment incomplete; no default/partly parsed classification is used. A malformed declared docs-impact root also blocks deliberately, with its original setup diagnostic visible; clean absent roots remain supported. |
| AC-04 | Resolve synthetic overlapping PRs with identical, contradictory, already-fixed and stale hunks. Verify every disposition, exact selected subset, changed-approval invalidation and fresh-base reconstruction. Test full exact-head Git diffs for large proposals, absent REST patches, unreadable/truncated inputs, changed heads and binary file dispositions; incomplete proposals cannot be applied or closed and cannot disappear from the selected scope. Test positive superseded closure with every hunk accounted for and a linked merged consolidation, an uncarried deferred/undecided hunk staying open, explicit merged carry-forward, missing feedback, and closure attempts before publication, after publication but before merge, and after the consolidation closes unmerged; changed proposal/consolidation bindings stop the sequence. Exercise consumer-allowed squash and rebase merges using GET head equality and exact postimage blobs rather than reviewed-head ancestry; a later unrelated in-scope edit requires fresh closure-only assessment, and a later revert or removed carry-forward keeps the affected original open. Start post-merge closure in a fresh session/second host holding only the merged PR ID and current authority; load the reviewed-tree record, freshly review checkpoint/current default/body/effects, and complete only the eligible approved closures. Missing/ambiguous records or changed bindings deny; private historical artifacts are unnecessary and a PR-body approval claim grants nothing. A consolidation updated by merging advanced default is explicitly unsupported and denies closure; test that case and the supported fresh-clean-candidate/review route when branch protection requires an up-to-date head. A disjoint default advance without a head update remains supported under its normal read-set check. Add a commit to the consolidation after its publication review and merge it: unlisted paths or changed postimages deny fresh-session closure, rather than comparing the observed head to itself. Test an unattended follow-up without an adopted post-merge grant and require no write. Test narrow detector suppression versus interpretation correction, no feedback writes in report mode, repeated mistakes and incomplete-scan refusal to retire suppressions. Test closure-only retry after later default input or consolidation-body changes: it may pass only with complete comparison, unchanged original bindings, verified immutable merged postimages/dispositions and carried decisions plus fresh exact explanation approval. Changed original head/body or an unlisted/mismatched merged diff leaves originals open with recovery, and no closure-only record rewrite can make it pass. A differing historical head with the same exact permitted diff is checked afresh without claiming recovery of the old review; a change after fresh review invalidates the batch. Exercise an all-already-fixed selection and wrong-with-feedback-already-present selection: an authorized resolve produces only the pre-review record plus exact reviewed PR dispositions; no original closes before that record-only consolidation merges and passes fresh closure checks. Without publication authority, keep the proposal local and originals open. For squash, rebase and merge-commit histories, insert an in-scope default commit after publication but before merge that invalidates an already-fixed disposition or an applied hunk's source evidence without changing the consolidation postimages. Full recorded-base-to-current-default comparison must detect it and require fresh disposition verification, keeping the affected original open when no longer satisfied. Test an unrelated disjoint advance as a passing control, post-merge changes separately and unavailable comparison/history as refusal; only exact declared postimages and unchanged record count as the expected transition. |
| AC-05 | Test the single publisher through native CLI wiring and injected synthetic collector, denying production collector selection; observe-before-replay lost PR/issue responses, comment-before-close interruption, unavailable state, lost artifacts and unauthorized replay without duplicates. Inspect actual model reports for concise summary, complete hidden/detail evidence, recommendations, unknowns and precise next decisions. Test unrelated and in-scope default-branch advances using the declared read-set/history rule. After cutover race shared scheduled/local publishers through the same lease; deny a foreign or interrupted lease, and allow only atomic exact-owned release, denying a replaced lease. Read committed/current-default settings from exact Git blob bytes through the strict byte reader, including when that default is not checked out. Read the committed current-default publication gate before each selected remote effect; missing/staged state and stale adopted local configuration after rollback must deny shared publication. Verify exact context/payload/client provenance and label these as synthetic procedure/API coverage. Production native transport rejects collector flags/contexts and ignores report/settings/environment attempts to select it; the trial driver rejects real repository IDs, network endpoints, foreign paths and out-of-namespace effects. Cover native production command construction separately and retain the hosted live advisory trial requirement. Incomplete scans, should-fix reviews, blocked effects, pending recovery and upload failure remain red even with matching old attention. Test an artifact planted by another workflow, a fork/PR run, unapproved source, wrong attempt/context, expired artifact, changed archive bytes and malformed/oversized ZIP; none may suppress attention. A complete prior new-attention report from a verified producer can carry on the next run despite its red conclusion, while prior incomplete/technical-failure reports cannot. Ref creation needs exact grant; reject a substituted ref/object and reconcile lost create responses without duplicates or deletions. Test receipt create/retain with absent namespace grant, and retirement with absent cleanup grant, a foreign/unrecorded ref or a changed object: no such mutation occurs. Retire only exact recorded owned refs under the separately selected cleanup scope after linked needs finish. The run must list the stale proposal as needs-resolution/new-attention, never clean no-change or replay; a matching subsequent complete report may carry that unresolved decision. Reject a collector success projection in a native or production plan, mismatched transport/context labels and relabelled native/collector evidence. Interrupt after successful dispatch but before artifact persistence or journal linking, leaving the unproved attempt pending. Verify the effect-specific marker/baseline and direct application proof, exact repository/actor/target/head bindings, current field digests, original journal/run and lease nonce, and per-request native settlement. Preserve the edited resource and retained intent ref, record the separate non-success event and release only that owned lease. Interrupt before/after event publication and around release intent/dispatch/response, including concurrent approvals and a replacement holder. Resume without duplicate acknowledgement or business replay and keep non-success after payload restoration. An installed resolve collector case must prove the expected non-success acknowledgement plus exact cleanup and unchanged remote fixture; a generic failure cannot count as its passing result. |

A required unattended trial remains pending until observed; static worker checks and an interactive run cannot satisfy it. |

## Tasks

Code tasks add and run the regression before the behavior change. Each task is one coherent commit with its observed checks recorded below it. Publication and live-operation tasks retain the explicit authority stated in their done conditions.

Extract and reuse the hardened Git primitive. Focused Git and existing docs-impact tests pass. (AC-02, AC-03)
      Evidence: `bun test tests/vh-adlc/git-read.test.ts` first failed because the neutral module was absent. Both manifests now select 0.11.0, and `bun test tests/vh-adlc/check.test.ts tests/vh-adlc/audit/cli.test.ts --timeout 30000` passed 66 tests. Registration checks passed 34 tests. The initial broad Bun run remains diagnostic, with its failures retained for final-gate reconciliation. Manual documentation-impact review found the extraction internal to collection, with the isolated-copy dependency list updated. Logs are retained under `.docs-drift-review-archive/t1-*` outside the published payload.

### R-57

### R-60

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: Design, Authority and delivery, the receipt-ref and trial-cleanup paragraphs (chunk 00290 line 13; chunk 00211 line 11); Tasks T23, "Main freshness on 2026-11-13" (chunk 01059 line 7)
EVIDENCE: Every publisher creates a retained receipt ref under `publication.receipt_ref_prefix`, "for example `refs/heads/docs-drift-receipts/<operation-id>`". That ref is an evidence-only commit that is never merged. "Retirement needs separately authorized cleanup after every linked proposal/recovery need is finished". The authority paragraph also grants "exact-owned trial PR/branch cleanup" for synthetic draft-PR branches that are never merged. The current pinned delivery convention at d8f07c9 says "Before deleting a stale branch, prove its current tip is merged" and "Do not delete the default branch, protected branches, active PR branches, or branches with unpublished or unmerged commits", and "Report any branch that cannot safely be removed" (delivery.md 00001 lines 12-24). Read literally, receipt retirement and trial-branch cleanup delete branches with unmerged commits. The integrated main drift also adds a stale-branch sweep to every maintenance run. It keeps "unmerged work" and reports each kept branch with its reason (workflows.md 00211 lines 1-24; authority.md 00001 row "Cleaning stale merged branches"). Retained `refs/heads/docs-drift-receipts/*` branches would therefore show up in every consumer sweep until they are retired. T23 says the new pin updates "delivery/updater/cleanup conventions" but reports only that "The applicable new page is read". It does not reconcile the updated cleanup rule with these two owned-ref deletions or with the new sweep output. The settled behavior is not wrong: the refs hold no authored work. The gap is only that the conflict with the pinned rule goes unrecorded.
PROPOSAL: Record in Context or T23, without changing the accepted normative sections, that exact-owned, evidence-only receipt refs and synthetic trial branches hold no authored work. Say that their separately granted retirement or cleanup is an intended exception to the delivery stale-branch rule, and that maintenance sweeps will list retained receipt branches as kept unmerged branches. If the owner would rather move receipts out of the `refs/heads/` example namespace, handle that through the normal revision and re-acceptance boundary.

### R-61


FINDING: F-01

That sentence would say that evidence-only receipt refs and synthetic trial branches are an intended exception to the pinned delivery rule against stale unmerged branches, and that maintenance sweeps list them as kept unmerged branches. The R-60 Review Log row calls this "one cleanup-rule context nit". The current document does not record it. Context, the T23 status and integration notes, and the later T23 notes have no statement about the exception or the sweep listing. The only text on it is the R-60 record itself. The pinned delivery convention still requires stale branches to be deleted or recorded.
PROPOSAL: Add the sentence R-60 proposed to Context or the T23 notes. Alternatively, record in the Review Log that the nit was knowingly deferred and why.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: shared/tests/vh-adlc/docs-drift/role-process.test.ts, test.each "reopened new capture rechecks native launch and actual final reply" for the removed-invocation, legacy-downgrade and invocation-cwd cases
EVIDENCE: All three new negative cases end with a bare `.toThrow()` (line 125 in the first-red log; the same call is at test 00086 line 40). readNativeRoleCapture has a distinct refusal for each fault. A schema 2 capture with no invocation fails with "modern invocation record required". A legacy capture under require_invocation fails with "modern invocation capture required". A cwd mismatch throws ArtifactChanged "docs-drift original role working directory changed". The test does not check which refusal fires, so the cases would still pass if an unrelated earlier failure triggered, such as a resealed digest or binding mismatch. The cwd fault would also pass if it lost its ArtifactChanged classification, which audit-session relies on to report scope change. role-capture.test.ts asserts exact messages for the same reader.
PROPOSAL: Give each case an expected message. Assert `.toThrow(message)` for all three and `toThrow(ArtifactChanged)` for invocation-cwd, in the style of the role-capture.test.ts table.

FINDING: F-03
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: shared/plugins/vh-adlc/scripts/docs-drift/audit-session.ts, reopen helper (`expected_cwd: cwd`, where `cwd = baseline.scan.checkout`); role-process.ts lines 61 and 78; role-capture.ts line 76
EVIDENCE: role-process records `invocation.cwd: realpathSync(options.cwd)` and reopens with `expected_cwd: realpathSync(options.cwd)`. audit-session passes the raw `baseline.scan.checkout` string. role-capture compares the two strings exactly and throws ArtifactChanged. If scan.checkout is ever a non-canonical path to the same directory, every audit-session reopen with require_invocation fails as "role working directory changed". That would be a false scope-change refusal even though the capture is intact. The required scope does not show that scan.checkout is canonical. Only one retained session case covers this path, and the test fixtures pass canonical paths (`expected_cwd: f.repo`), so a mismatch would not show up.
PROPOSAL: Canonicalize in one place. Either pass `realpathSync(cwd)` in audit-session's reopen, or require that scan.checkout equal its realpath at the point where audit-session binds it.

### R-62

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Audit and preparation, the feature-dialect sentence (chunk 00181 line 9). Test Plan AC-03 (chunk 00458 line 19). Tasks T23, "Upstream loop reconciliation and component111" (chunk 00995 line 5). Open Questions ("None.").
EVIDENCE: The accepted Design says the feature dialect is added "preserving the gate's `internal/doc/covers.go:MatchCovers` semantics". It calls double-star "unsupported" and reports it as a gap. AC-03 tests the feature dialect "against Go MatchCovers shared fixtures". After merge 13b789c, upstream MatchCovers matches `**` through compileDoubleStar, and lint now accepts valid `**` covers in feature documents. t23-upstream-loop-covers-native-first-red.log shows the Go matcher returning true for `src/**`, `src/**/` and `src/p**x.ts`. matching.ts still rejects them, and the reconciliation adds gate_matched overrides so that Go parity is asserted only for the non-`**` subset. So a feature document whose valid `**` covers pass the gate is now reported by docs drift as a coverage gap. The Design text still promises the gate's semantics. The reconciliation is test-only and keeps the accepted conservative behavior, which is sound. The only record of the divergence is T23 Task prose. Open Questions says "None." and the owner summary does not mention it. review-protocol.md routes judgement findings to Open Questions rather than to silent prose.
PROPOSAL: Add an Open Question for the owner without editing the accepted normative text. Option (a): keep the conservative feature subset, and at the next re-acceptance revise the Design and AC-03 wording to say "the path.Match subset of MatchCovers; double-star patterns the gate accepts remain conservative gaps". Option (b): extend the feature dialect so valid `**` follows the gate's current semantics, giving full parity. Until the owner chooses, keep the gate_matched overrides and the current TS behavior. Mention the divergence in the Owner summary.

### R-63

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, standard feature-document protection paragraph (normative-revision.patch 00052 line 3, "Reuse the existing double-star compilation in the shared matcher"); Test Plan AC-03 (patch 00063 line 2); T91 (spec 00828 line 11).
EVIDENCE: Go MatchCovers checks `strings.Contains(c, "**")` before the directory-suffix and literal branches (covers.go 00001 lines 18 to 35). It sends every such pattern, including escaped forms like `src/\**` and directory forms like `src/**/`, to compileDoubleStar. That function strips leading `./`, works on runes, gives `?` the meaning `[^/]`, and makes classes that never match `/`. Its own `coverageClass` comment says so, and the doc comment says these patterns follow the docs-impact matcher. The path.Match branch behaves differently: matching.ts runs `feature` input as bytes and lets classes consume `/` (`matches = (feature || char !== "/") && ...`, 00086 line 25), and the fixtures "class consumes slash" and "negated class consumes slash" confirm this. So simply dropping the `**` rejection while keeping `dialect === "feature"` would answer `a[^x]**` against `a/b` with true, where Go returns false. The vectors the revision requires cannot catch this. covers-cases.json has no `**` pattern whose class or `?` sits next to `/` or a multibyte rune. The existing feature fixtures test slash-consuming classes only without `**`. Neither the Design nor AC-03 says that `**` patterns take the documentation token semantics wholesale, or that `**` routing comes first and uses the raw substring.
PROPOSAL: In Design, say that any declaration containing `**` follows MatchCovers' order and uses the documentation-dialect compilation unchanged. That includes `./` normalization, rune input, and `?` and classes that exclude `/`. Other patterns keep path.Match semantics. In AC-03 and T91, add distinguishing vectors that compare the TS dialect with actual Go. Include a class and a negated class beside `/` in a `**` pattern, `?` against a multibyte rune in a `**` pattern, `src/\**`, and `src/**/` being routed before the directory-suffix check.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: T91 file list (spec 00828 line 11); Test Plan AC-03 ("the ordinary feature-ownership protection path").
EVIDENCE: T91 must "verify ... ordinary ownership protection". AC-03 requires the feature-ownership protection path. But T91 names only matching.ts, feature-covers.test.ts, feature-covers.json and covers_test.go. In protection.ts (00086 lines 72 to 85, 00171 lines 1 to 12), a pattern that throws turns the whole assessment into a `feature-classification` gap. A matching pattern feeds `affected_features` and `active-feature-overlap`. None of the named files exercises that path with a `**` feature declaration. The first-red the task requires is also only a compile-level failure.
PROPOSAL: Add the protection test file that T91 will change to the task's file list. Name its red/green case: a standard feature with `covers: [src/**]` today blocks the assessment as a classification gap, and after parity it reports the overlapping guide as affected or claimed.

FINDING: F-03
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: Owner summary, second paragraph (patch 00001 line 47, "This revision adds that requirement and its tests for Copilot review").
EVIDENCE: The revision changes only the spec. The delta lists Design, AC-03, covers and the new T91. Current source still has the conservative feature dialect (`if (normalized.includes("**")) malformed();`), the three `gate_matched` overrides are still in feature-covers.json, and T91 is unchecked. No tests are added yet. Only Test Plan requirements are.
PROPOSAL: Change the sentence to "This revision adds that requirement and its Test Plan for Copilot review; T91 adds the tests after acceptance."

### R-64

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: Test Plan AC-03, the distinguishing-vector sentence (normative-revision.patch 00060 line 2, "`**/*??` against a single multibyte rune, `**/?.md` against a multibyte-rune filename"); T91 vector list (spec 00828 line 11); T91 "R63 clarification" paragraph ("distinguishing vectors explicit").
EVIDENCE: The revision gives these two vectors to show that the `**` branch uses rune input rather than the feature dialect's byte input. Neither does that as written. In matching.ts the feature dialect is already rune-aware for `?` and classes. It works out each position's decoded rune width (00001 lines 66 to 72), and the `one` token advances by that width (00086 line 14). Only `*` advances by bytes. So `**/?.md` against a filename such as `é.md` returns true on both branches, and Go's `(?:.*/)?[^/]\.md$` also returns true. It cannot distinguish the two branches. For `**/*??` the result depends on the rune's byte length. With a 2-byte rune such as `é` (c3 a9), the byte-input branch also returns false. A zero-byte star leaves `?` (width 2) and then nothing for the second `?`. A one-byte star leaves one replacement-width `?` and again nothing for the second. That equals Go's rune answer, so this vector also fails to distinguish. Only a rune of three or more bytes separates them, such as the existing fixture's `🚀` ("byte star followed by runes", `*??` true on the byte branch) or `€`. In that case the byte branch returns true and Go returns false. As written, T91 could add both vectors, see them pass with byte input kept for `**` declarations, and leave the Design's required rune input untested. No current loop or feature vector combines `**` with a multibyte path. The other listed vectors do distinguish: `a[/]**` and `a[^x]**` against `a/b` are false in Go and true through the slash-consuming feature class, and `src/**/` against a nested file distinguishes routing order. The three task-history chunks read since then (00604, 00617, 00630: T79 evidence and T80 to T90, T78) are completed historical records and do not affect the coverage revision.
PROPOSAL: In AC-03 and T91, require `**/*??` against a single rune of at least three UTF-8 bytes, for example `🚀`, with the expected Go answer false. Either drop `**/?.md` from the distinguishing list or label it a positive control. Keep the Design text unchanged, because its rune-input requirement already matches compileDoubleStar.

### R-65

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: Frontmatter status_description (chunk 00001 line 3).
EVIDENCE: The line reads "T92 current-source proposal verification and carried delivery pass 59 selected regressions, repository checks and retained-CLI cases. Its scoped committed-source Copilot review is next." feature-documents.md (chunk 00001 line 35) says status_description gives the current state and what it waits for. The current evidence names a different next step. current-fresh.json marks R-64 stale because of covered upstream drift in the two plugin.json files, which arrived with the merge of main 957db34 at 656a9a1. current-next.json gives the next action as spec review. current-gate.json fails only on spec_review_fresh. The status line does not mention the stale accepted review or the spec re-review, so a reader would think only the implementation review remains. R-64 F-02 (chunk 01176) corrected this field for a similar mismatch. The other claims check out. The T92 summaries add up to the stated totals: 29+8+13+2+6 = 58 cases with 178+101+140+77+57 = 553 assertions, plus 1 case with 20 assertions, for 59 cases and 573 assertions. The package and registration stages report 64 and 34 cases. Every stage passed, and the head, hashes and git status were unchanged. normative-equality.json confirms the accepted Scope, Design, AC, Test Plan and covers are byte-identical to the R-64 baseline. I found no concrete defects in the scoped source and tests: matching.ts, protection.ts, current-proposal-verification.ts, audit-proposal-report.ts, audit-report.ts and the listed tests.
PROPOSAL: Change the line to "T92 current-source proposal verification and carried delivery pass 59 selected regressions, repository checks and retained-CLI cases. Covered drift from main 957db34 (plugin.json versions) makes R-64 stale pending spec re-review, followed by T92's scoped committed-source Copilot review. Final reporting, operator and migration work remain open."

## Sources

- [Git revision traversal](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-rev-list)
- [GitHub workflow runs API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/workflow-runs) and [GitHub CLI run listing](https://cli.github.com/manual/gh_run_list) identify native run status and conclusion values used in quiescence records.

- [curl manual](https://curl.se/docs/manpage.html) documents transfer/connection limits, disabled retries and `-q`. The installer makes one bounded attempt per route and excludes user curl configuration.

- [Earlier pinned feature-document standard](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/conventions/feature-documents.md) and [template](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/guides/feature-document-template.md): one child document, owner decisions, review and status gates.
- [Earlier pinned documentation conventions](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/conventions/documentation.md), [delivery rules](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/conventions/delivery.md) and [prose guide](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/guides/humanize-prose.md): publication boundaries, authoritative local rules and concise owner-facing prose.
- [Earlier pinned agent entry point](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/AGENTS.md): source-grounded task routing and retained consumer instructions.
- Stackweaver files cited in Context were inspected locally at `d77259358c87aebc93ad76231ae245fd790c043a`; private report bodies, application source and secrets are not copied into this draft.

- [GitHub local plugin directories](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/how-tos/copilot-sdk/features/plugin-directories), [CLI configuration directory](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/copilot-cli-reference/cli-config-dir-reference) and [programmatic reference](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/copilot-cli-reference/cli-programmatic-reference), consulted 2026-11-05: explicit plugin loading, isolated configuration and update controls. Local CLI help confirms `--plugin-dir`, `COPILOT_HOME` and automatic-update controls; actual new-operation loading remains a required test.
- [GitHub Actions artifacts REST API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/artifacts): artifact IDs, expiry/digest/run metadata, archive retrieval and Actions read permission for private resources used by the proposed history reader.
- [GitHub Actions workflow runs REST API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/workflow-runs): native workflow/event/branch/head/attempt metadata used to distinguish adopted scheduled producer runs from other workflows and pull-request runs.
- [yauzl 3.4.0 source](https://github.com/thejoshwolfe/yauzl/tree/f5798e15204ffbb9a428b00217e4659684a41b0f): exact tagged ZIP-library source selected for bundling; its documented buffer/lazy-entry/filename/size-validation APIs support the proposed bounded wrapper. Package installation and integrity remain implementation verification.

- [Official yauzl 3.4.0 npm metadata](https://registry.npmjs.org/yauzl/3.4.0) and [Bun 1.4.4 release](https://github.com/oven-sh/bun/releases/tag/bun-v1.4.4). T10 records registry integrity, exact upstream-source comparison and verified Linux build-tool execution.

- [GitHub workflow jobs API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/workflow-jobs). T12 uses the fixed run-attempt jobs endpoint and native step outcomes under the existing Actions read permission. The run API examples also document branch-qualified workflow paths, which are checked against the recorded default branch or exact native head.

- [Earlier engineering entry point](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/f781ee7b0404e80194aa568af3284888cc222017/AGENTS.md) and [owner-summary guidance](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/f781ee7b0404e80194aa568af3284888cc222017/conventions/feature-documents.md). T40 integrates the published upstream pin and adds a summary and glossary of the accepted behavior.

GitHub documents that the actual `merge_commit_sha` after a merge identifies the resulting merge commit, squashed commit or rebased default tip. T51 reads that identity separately from a hypothetical pre-merge test commit. [GitHub pull-request REST documentation](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/pulls/pulls#get-a-pull-request)

Git documents all-or-none ref updates for an atomic push and failure when the server lacks that capability. T52 verifies those outcomes against an owned local receive-pack server. [Git push documentation](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-push)

- [GitHub labels REST API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/issues/labels#get-a-label) provides the repository label-identity lookup used to compare native canonical spelling. Read-only GETs for `cli/cli` labels `bug` and `BUG` returned the same label ID and canonical name during implementation.

- [Git reference reads](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/git/refs#get-a-reference) and [Git commit object reads](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/git/commits#get-a-commit-object) use repository Contents read permission. Native ref absence rechecks the current publication repository identity, default ref and native commit, retaining those requests before classifying visible absence.

- Bun's archive API provides in-memory regular-file reads and tar extraction. [Bun archive documentation](https://bun.sh/docs/runtime/archive).
- Copilot exposes explicit configuration/cache locations and update suppression for scoped probes. [Copilot CLI configuration directory](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/copilot-cli-reference/cli-config-dir-reference), [Copilot programmatic reference](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/copilot-cli-reference/cli-programmatic-reference).
- Claude exposes separate configuration and update controls for the version-only probe. [Claude Code environment variables](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/env-vars).

The bounded client-distribution reader uses Bun for regular tar files and the runtime zlib API for capped gzip expansion. It validates ustar metadata separately so omitted entries cannot become an accepted package. Extended archive formats require an explicitly supported setup path. [Bun archive reference](https://bun.sh/docs/runtime/archive), [Node zlib options](https://nodejs.org/api/zlib.html#class-options)

Non-executing client upgrade signals use the documented provider npm package names as intake identifiers. Runtime selection continues to use independently pinned native distributions. [Copilot CLI installation](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/get-started/cli-quickstart), [Claude Code setup](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/setup), [Codex source installation guide](https://github.com/openai/codex#installation)

- [Current engineering entry point](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/d8f07c9dd69038375a535af8ad586086688f85ab/AGENTS.md), [terminal output](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/d8f07c9dd69038375a535af8ad586086688f85ab/conventions/terminal-output.md) and [delivery rules](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/d8f07c9dd69038375a535af8ad586086688f85ab/conventions/delivery.md). The preserving upstream integration selects this pin through the existing T40 scope and keeps the binary lock unchanged.
